# Engine 2a-2: CEL Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Replace 2a-1's `cel.unavailable` diagnostic with working CEL: typed environments at publish, classification
(reject / local / activity) with static bounds stored per version, deterministic in-process evaluation, and the
isolated, secretless `cel-evaluator` service, all behind the §5.9 gates.

**Architecture:** `dewpoint.engine.cel` is pure. Only `runtime.py` imports cel-expr-python (import-linter contract).
Publish walks each expression's checked AST (vendored cel-spec protos) to type its references, check scope and
guards, classify it and bound its cost. The result is an `ExpressionRecord` stored in
`workflow_versions.expressions`. At run time (wired into `RunGraph` in 2a-3), a record plus a `ScopeView` gives the
bindings, the routing decision and the outcome. Activity-class expressions go over a length-prefixed Unix-socket
protocol to `apps/cel_evaluator`: a single-threaded zygote that forks one rlimited child per request.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12, `cel-expr-python==0.1.3` (Google cel-cpp), `protobuf` 7.x with vendored `cel.expr`
protos (cel-spec `b0b1083`, generated by grpcio-tools 1.84.0), asyncio Unix sockets, pytest + Hypothesis. Docker
Compose and a Linux CI container job run the gates.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-engine-core-design.md`, revision 5.3: §5 (CEL), §4.3 (scope), §3
(output schemas), §10 (testing), §12. Revision 5.3 records the decisions below; it lands in the same docs-only PR as
this plan, so the spec and the plan agree before implementation starts.

**Previous plan:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-engine-2a1-contracts-graph-versions.md` (merged, PR #5).
2a-1's split table assigns this plan: *runtime adapter, typed environment (typed-path proof first), `fn-1`,
classifier, bound estimator, canonicalization, `cel-evaluator` service, CEL gates; replaces this plan's
`cel.unavailable` diagnostic.*

## How this plan was made

The owner asked for list-field type propagation to be the first go/no-go experiment, resolved before the function
library or the evaluator was designed. So:

1. The experiment ran first, against the spike's installed `cel-expr-python` 0.1.3. The results are in the next
   section: **go**, with conditions.
2. Every module in this plan was then prototyped in a scratch copy of `backend/` (nothing was downloaded; the spike's
   environment supplied the runtime) until it passed its tests, `ruff`, `ruff format`, `mypy --strict` and
   `lint-imports`. The totals were 461 tests (engine, evaluator and SDK suites), plus 8 that run only on Linux.
3. The code blocks below are those files, verbatim. Diffs to 2a-1 files were generated from the prototype, one stage
   per task, and each stage was tested on its own.
4. The owner reviewed it on 2026-09-26 and tightened two decisions: guards for schema-declared optional fields
   (decision 4) and the evaluator's Kubernetes transport (decision 13). The guard rule exposed a 2a-1 false positive,
   now fixed by Task 7 (decision 16). Both were prototyped the same way before this revision.
5. Execution revised it at the owner's checkpoints, and the code blocks follow the branch:
   - checkpoint 2: typed field serializers (decision 16);
   - checkpoint 3: `map`/`filter` memory grows with the square of the range. The owner chose local caps of 200, a
     4 MiB memory limit and a charge for retained accumulators (decision 7). The same gates showed that the work
     bound missed calls whose cost grows with what they read, now charged at measured rates (decision 6). Every
     charge is mutation-checked by gate 6 or 7 (Task 12). The first Linux run measured no memory growth at all (an
     earlier peak hid it), so the child now resets its peak and a blind measurement fails. Linux then measured
     every margin (Task 12's Expected lines); the owner finalizes the numbers from it.

Not run during planning: the Linux-only tests (real rlimits), the database-backed tests of Task 9, the Docker image,
the Compose service and the CI job (Tasks 14–15). The executor runs them; Expected lines say so.

## Go/no-go: list-field type propagation (run 2026-09-26)

Encoding under test (spec §5.3): roots are `map<string, dyn>`, and every always-available array path is also declared
as a qualified identifier with its list type, for example `steps.get_devices.output.devices : list<map<string, dyn>>`.

| # | Question | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does the checker resolve the longest declared qualified name, typing the comprehension range as a list? | **Yes**, for `steps`, `trigger`, `loops.<key>.item`, `list<string>`, and segments that are CEL reserved words such as `if`. Parentheses don't change it. |
| 2 | Do undeclared paths stay `dyn` (so the classifier can reject them)? | **Yes**. So do `x["key"]` index syntax and lists nested inside elements (`d.ports`): `asList()` is still needed there. |
| 3 | `has()` on roots and on prefixes of a typed path? | **Works** (`has(steps.a)`, `has(steps.a.output)`). |
| 4 | `has()` on the declared typed path itself? | **Collapses.** The checker rewrites `has(p)` into the identifier `p`: the result is the list, so `has(p) && …` is a type error, and a bare `has(p)` returns the list. Parsed and checked ASTs share node ids, so it's detectable. Condition: publish rejects it (`cel.has_on_typed_path`), and a `has()` test alone never declares a path. |
| 5 | Binding both the root map and the qualified identifier? | **Works.** The qualified binding is what the expression reads. A declared identifier that a short-circuit never reaches doesn't need a value. |
| 6 | Does an iteration variable named like a root shadow it, identically in checker and runtime? | **Yes** (`[…].map(steps, steps.x…)` reads the variable in both). |
| 7 | Does the runtime enforce declared types at binding? | **Yes, deeply** (a dict where `list<list<dyn>>` is declared fails at `rows[0]`). It also *converts*: `bool`→`int`, `int`→`double`, `bytes`→`string`, and refuses `3.0` in an `int` slot. Condition: our binder checks first (clear `type_mismatch`), and number elements stay `dyn`. |
| 8 | Compile cost with many declarations? | 2,000 declarations: environment about 5 ms, compile about 0.07 ms. Declarations are per expression anyway (only the expression's own chains). |
| 9 | Is the checked AST deterministic? | **The runtime's serialization isn't** (8 distinct byte strings in 20 compiles: protobuf map order). The decoded messages are equal, and `SerializeToString(deterministic=True)` is stable. Condition: nothing stores or hashes the runtime's bytes. |
| 10 | Reserved words? | **`loop` is a reserved identifier**: the spec's `loop.item` can't be a CEL root. `in`, `true`, `false`, `null` can't be selected as fields; every other reserved word can. Condition: decisions 1 and 2 below. |

**Verdict: go.** The typed-path encoding types list fields reliably. The `asList()` fallback stays in `fn-1` for
`dyn` and nested lists, not as a replacement. The conditions above are rules in Tasks 1–8; the proof is
`tests/engine/cel/test_typed_paths.py`, which also guards every future runtime upgrade.

Other facts pinned during planning, all with tests:
- The runtime's iteration budget lets **9,999** iterations through and fails at 10,000, including across several
  comprehensions. The spec's local bound of "≤ 10,000" would have let the budget fire locally.
- Exceeding the budget *raises* from `eval` (it isn't an error value), so `||` can't absorb it. A CEL error is an
  `ERROR`-typed value whose `plain_value()` is a string, so the adapter checks the type name.
- A Python exception in a custom function becomes an error value (`OUT_OF_RANGE: <message>`) that `||`/`&&` absorb.
  A `dyn` parameter doesn't match values at runtime, so `asList` is declared on `list<dyn>` and the dispatcher itself
  rejects non-lists.
- Every extension library, `cel.bind`, optional syntax (`.?`) and two-variable comprehensions are off by default.
- Checked-in protoc output from grpcio-tools 1.84.0 is byte-identical to the spike's generated files; the `.proto`
  sources match cel-spec commit `b0b10835ca4d31a1a32b86e2e25d66bb9dd8f042` exactly (git blob hashes).
- Cost on the planning machine: about 0.08 µs per native node evaluation, about 1.1 µs per Python-implemented
  `fn-1` call, 0.26 ms to bind 1,000 small maps.

## Decisions (recorded in spec revision 5.3)

Each decision goes beyond or against revision 5.2. Each is forced by a measured fact, or is the smallest choice that
satisfies the spec.

The owner's review on 2026-09-26:
- approved 1 (`item`/`index`), 5 (9,999 iterations), 6 (the work bound) and 12 (the Temporal-dependent gates move to
  2a-3);
- tightened 4 (guards) and 13 (the evaluator's transport);
- 16 came out of tightening 4.

The docs PR that carries this plan writes all of them into the spec.

1. **`item` and `index` replace `loop.item` and `loop.index`, in references and in CEL.** CEL reserves `loop`, so a
   `loop` root can't exist in expressions. `item` also names the filter item in a `filter` predicate, where 2a-1's
   own test already wrote `item > 1`. `loops.<key>.item` is unchanged. `loop.item` now fails with a message pointing
   to `item`. No published workflows exist yet, so nothing migrates. (Task 6)
2. **`in`, `true`, `false`, `null` are refused as step keys (`graph.reserved_key`) and as variable names
   (`vars.invalid_name`).** CEL can't select them as fields. (Task 6)
3. **Step presence contract.** `steps` holds every step visible from the scope: `{"output": …}` once it succeeded,
   `{"error": …}` after a handled failure, `{}` before either. The guard for a step is `has(steps.x.output)`, not
   `has(steps.x)`: a step that failed with `on_error: continue` exists but has no output. 2a-3 builds `ScopeView` to
   this contract. (Tasks 5, 8, 10)
4. **Guards follow what the schema promises** (tightened in review). `cel.conditional_ref` requires a `has()` guard
   for:
   - a step that may not have run (`has(steps.x.output)`);
   - every field the schema **declares but doesn't require**, and every such ancestor, e.g.
     `has(trigger.a) && has(trigger.a.b) && trigger.a.b.c == 1`.

   Data the schema doesn't declare (open objects, schemaless webhook bodies) needs no guard: if missing at run time,
   it's an `evaluation_error` that the step's error policy handles. Optional paths are never typed, so a guard never
   meets the `has()`-on-typed-path collapse. References keep their rule unchanged: a default for anything
   conditional. (Task 8)
5. **Local means ≤ 9,999 iterations**, not ≤ 10,000: the runtime fails at 10,000 (measured). (Task 5)
6. **A work bound, charged by what each call reads** (revised at checkpoint 3). Iterations don't bound CPU: a body
   calling a Python-implemented `fn-1` function 150 times per element costs about 0.2 s per evaluation at 1,000
   elements, with iterations still at 1,000. Copying a 16 KiB string, a substring search and a regular expression
   also cost far more than a node. The estimator adds *work*: one unit per node evaluation, plus each call's cost
   by the size of what it reads, at measured rates of at most about 40 ns a unit (spec §5.5):
   - an `fn-1` call: 15, plus the text (bytes/64) or values (bytes/16) it converts;
   - text copied or read (concatenation, `size`, conversions, comparisons, prefixes, map keys): bytes/64;
   - list and map equality (the smaller value) and `in` over a list: model bytes/16;
   - `contains`: text × needle / 2,048 (an 8 KiB needle in 16 KiB takes 1.3 ms);
   - `matches`: text × pattern code points / 4, with `{n,m}` counts written out. Patterns with `(?` groups or letter
     escapes beyond `\d \s \w \b \A \z` are activity: their compiled size outgrows their text
     (`[\p{L}]{40}c` takes 31 ms over 16 KiB);
   - text from the inputs is at most a string at the cap per reference, and at most the inputs' JSON over a range.

   Local requires ≤ 2M units. The yield policy also sums work, with a threshold of 4M, next to the spec's 20,000
   iterations / 8 MiB / 200 evaluations. Measured on macOS: the heaviest local load (regular expressions) yields
   after 2 evaluations and 0.28 s of CPU, against the 1 s target. Before these charges, string copies, searches and
   patterns reached 1.4–3.4 s. (Tasks 4, 10, 12)
7. **Lists and maps are capped at 200 entries for local evaluation, and retained accumulators are charged** (owner,
   checkpoint 3). cel-expr-python 0.1.3 copies a `map` or `filter` accumulator every iteration and keeps every copy
   until the evaluation ends, so peak memory grows with the square of the range: 1.2 / 4.8 / 20.5 / 90 MiB at
   250 / 500 / 1,000 / 2,000 elements (macOS).
   - The estimator charges `caps.ACCUMULATOR_SLOT` (48 bytes, measured 41–45) × n(n + 1)/2 per list-building step,
     summed over a chain.
   - Local memory is the largest value plus those copies, ≤ 4 MiB. Larger inputs route to the evaluator.
   - Maps share the cap because the estimator needs a count bound for `sortedKeys(m)` ranges; without it, every map
     iteration would be activity-class.
   - Provisional until the Linux gates measure them. (Tasks 4, 5, 12)
8. **The size model and the input-mass rule** (spec §5.5 names the estimator but not its algebra):
   - sizes are modelled bytes: 16 per scalar or container, plus text bytes. Proved: model ≤ 8 × canonical JSON + 8;
   - distinct input references share one input budget (`a.x + a.y` is one input's worth), overlapping ones count
     again (`s + s`);
   - values inside a comprehension are affine in the current element, whose sizes sum to the range's;
   - `m[k]` over `sortedKeys(m)` sums to `m`.

   Without these, `trigger.a + '-' + trigger.b` and `sortedKeys(m).map(k, m[k])` would be activity-class. (Task 4)
9. **Roots are projected.** A root is bound as what the expression's chains can observe (maps keep chained keys; a
   `has()` test keeps only the key). Otherwise any expression reading `steps.a.output.x` would carry every step's
   output into measurement and the evaluator request, and would route to the activity, or fail `input_too_large`,
   once a workflow has large outputs. (Task 10)
10. **New codes.**
    - Publish: `cel.invalid`, `cel.unknown_name`, `cel.bad_path`, `cel.has_on_typed_path`, `cel.type_mismatch` (the
      checked result type against the field), `cel.non_json_result`, `graph.reserved_key`. `cel.conditional_ref`
      covers both guards in decision 4. `cel.iteration_budget` is a warning (nested loops, or a bound over 9,999).
    - Run time: `evaluation_error` (any other CEL error: missing key, division by zero, bad argument; the spec listed
      none for these), `non_json_value` (§5.8 named it), `evaluation_crashed` (the child died by a signal other than
      the CPU limits). (Tasks 8, 11, 14)
11. **Evaluator requests carry 1 to 1,000 binding sets.** Each set is its own evaluation with its own budget, for the
    `filter` batches of spec §6. A batch shares one child and its limits. (Task 13)
12. **Gates split with 2a-3.** This plan ports gates 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and the in-process half of 7. Gate 4 (Temporal
    replay) and the workflow-task half of gate 7 need `RunGraph`, so they run in 2a-3. `LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE` stays
    `None` here, which the spec's rollout requires until all seven pass. (Tasks 1, 12)
13. **The evaluator's transport: Compose in 2a, Kubernetes deferred** (tightened in review). The evaluator and the
    CEL worker are two containers on one host sharing a Unix socket on a volume: a same-host design.
    - Separate Kubernetes pods (spec §5.7's "own Deployment") can't share that socket.
    - A sidecar in the worker's pod would share the worker's network namespace, and with it the worker's egress. That
      breaks the evaluator's no-egress boundary.

    So the Helm chart (sub-project 4) doesn't ship CEL evaluation until a network transport is designed and approved:
    - authenticated: mTLS or equivalent, with a credential that grants only evaluator calls and reaches nothing else;
    - a NetworkPolicy that admits only the CEL activity worker's pods and denies all egress;
    - no Kubernetes API access: `automountServiceAccountToken: false` and `enableServiceLinks: false`, since injected
      service variables would also trip the evaluator's environment allow-list.

    Until then, Kubernetes deployments can't run activity-class CEL. (Tasks 14–16)
14. **Compose deployment details** (spec §5.7 left them open):
    - the socket is mode 0666 on a volume only the evaluator and the CEL worker mount (they run as different users);
    - the environment allow-list includes variables the Python base image sets (`GPG_KEY`, `PYTHON_VERSION`,
      `PYTHON_SHA256`);
    - SIGKILL at the hard CPU limit maps to `cpu_limit`. (Tasks 14–15)
15. **`macNormalize` returns Mist's form** (12 lowercase hex digits, no separators). IP, CIDR and MAC arguments over
    64 code points are errors. (Task 2)
16. **The output contract is exact** (tightened in the second review). Output schemas are what references, guards
    and typed paths trust.
    - **Every serialized field is required.** Pydantic's serialization schema leaves defaulted fields out of
      `required`, yet a dump always emits them: a 2a-1 false positive for references, and one for decision 4's
      guards. The exceptions keep pydantic's answer: `TypedDict` keys that aren't required, and fields with
      `exclude_if`.
    - **By alias.** The schema names fields by alias, so outputs become JSON only through
      `dewpoint.sdk.dump_output`: `model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)`, no `exclude_*`. 2a-3's activity wrapper
      uses it.
    - **No self-serializing models.** A `@model_serializer` anywhere in an output can drop or rename promised fields,
      so `node_manifest` (and with it registration) refuses it.
    - **Typed field serializers** (checkpoint 2). A field serializer must declare its return type, which becomes the
      field's schema. One without is refused: the schema would keep the field's own type (`value: integer` emitted
      as `"v-1"`). Pydantic's serializers for its own types (dates, paths, addresses, URLs, secrets) are trusted.

    No `flow` or `testkit` contract changes (checked). (Task 7)

## Global Constraints

- Python ≥ 3.12; `cel-expr-python==0.1.3` exactly; `protobuf>=7.35.1,<8` (the vendored gencode is 7.35.1).
- `cel_profile` stays `cel-cpp-0.1.3/fn-1/cls-1` (`CURRENT_CEL_PROFILE`, migration 0007's `INITIAL_CEL_PROFILE`).
  `cls-1` is the classifier as this plan ships it: the checkpoint revisions (decisions 6, 7) don't bump it, because
  2a-1 refused CEL at publish and nothing was released (spec §5.1). Once 2a-2 is merged, a change to the caps, the
  estimator or the classifier's limits is `cls-2`.
  `ENGINE_ABI` stays `1`; `LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE` stays `None`.
- `dewpoint.engine` is pure: no DB, network or clock. Only `dewpoint.engine.cel.runtime` imports `cel_expr_python`.
  `dewpoint.engine.cel` imports neither `dewpoint.engine.graph`, `jsonschema` nor `pydantic`.
- `dewpoint.apps.cel_evaluator` imports only the standard library, `dewpoint.engine.cel`, `dewpoint.engine.canonical`
  and the runtime. It holds no secrets and makes no network calls.
- Limits (spec §5.2, §5.6, §5.7):
  - expressions ≤ 16,384 code points (2a-1 already refuses longer);
  - local: ≤ 4,096 code points, comprehension depth ≤ 1, ≤ 9,999 iterations, ≤ 4 MiB model bytes (the largest value
    plus retained accumulators), ≤ 2,000,000 work (charged by what calls read);
  - caps: 64 KiB canonical JSON per value and in total, 200 list elements, 200 map entries, 16 KiB per string (the
    caps, the memory limit and the work rates are provisional until the Linux gates);
  - output ≤ 256 KiB canonical JSON; requests ≤ 4 MiB; responses ≤ 256 KiB + 16 KiB;
  - child: `RLIMIT_AS` 256 MiB, `RLIMIT_CPU` 5 s (hard 6), `RLIMIT_NOFILE` 16, `RLIMIT_FSIZE` 0, `RLIMIT_CORE` 0, 5 s
    wall clock;
  - N = min(CPU, ⌊(memory − 256 MiB) / 264.25 MiB⌋), lowered by `pids.max − 8`, with a wait queue of N;
  - yield: 20,000 iterations, 8 MiB, 4,000,000 work or 200 evaluations since the last await.
- CEL guards: `has()` for steps that may not have run and for schema-declared optional fields and their optional
  ancestors; none for undeclared or schemaless data (decision 4).
- Map iteration order never reaches a value: every comprehension over a range not proven a list is rejected, and
  every output is canonicalized.
- **Carried forward from 2a-1 (ruling): a reference default replaces both a missing and a `null` value.** Here that
  means:
  - typing: a nullable path is never typed, and loop items reached through a reference with a default are typed from
    the widened schema;
  - run time: 2a-3's value resolver applies it (see the handoff).
- **Not in this plan:** the `collect` behaviour for failed iterations (2a-3 decides), and branch cleanup (none; keep
  `feat/foundations`, `spike/cel-evaluation`, `docs/engine-core-spec` and `feat/engine-2a1`).
- Conventions:
  - SPDX header on every source file; comments explain why;
  - commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`;
  - run the checks and the commit in one `&&` chain;
  - never push, open a PR or enable auto-merge without the owner's word.
- Checks (from `backend/`):
  - `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check . && uv run mypy src && uv run lint-imports`;
  - tests: `uv run pytest -q <paths>`. The full suite needs Docker for Postgres; `tests/engine/**` and
    `tests/apps/cel_evaluator/**` don't.

## The classifier, the estimator and the isolation model at a glance

**Publish** (`engine/graph/cel_check.py`, per CEL value). It runs these steps in order:
1. Parse, and collect the select chains.
2. Declare the roots, plus the typed prefixes of the expression's own chains.
3. Refuse `has()` on a typed path.
4. Type-check. Map the checker's errors to `cel.invalid`, `cel.unknown_name`, `cel.unknown_function` and
   `ref.loop_outside`.
5. Reject order leaks (`cel.map_iteration`, `cel.unproven_list`).
6. Check every chain's scope with the 2a-1 resolver (`ref.*`, `cel.bad_path`), and the guards of decision 4:
   steps that may not have run, and schema-declared optional fields and their optional ancestors
   (`cel.conditional_ref`).
7. Check the result type against the field.
8. Classify, and store an `ExpressionRecord`: declarations, identifiers, projections, mode, reason, iterations,
   bytes and work.

**Classification** (`engine/cel/classify.py` + `estimate.py`):
- Reject: parse/type errors, order leaks, `has()` on a typed path.
- Local only when all of these hold:
  - ≤ 4,096 code points;
  - comprehension depth ≤ 1;
  - every AST node and every overload has a size rule in the estimator's table (the allow-list *is* that table);
  - regexes with literal patterns ≤ 256 code points, without `(?` groups or letter escapes beyond
    `\d \s \w \b \A \z`;
  - time zones only UTC or fixed offsets;
  - iterations ≤ 9,999, bytes ≤ 4 MiB (retained accumulators included), work ≤ 2M.
- Everything else is activity-class.

**Run time** (2a-3 wires it):
1. `bind(record, ScopeView)` projects the roots, checks types and plain JSON, and measures against the caps.
2. `route()` chooses local only if all of these hold: the build's `LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE` equals the version's profile,
   the record is local, and the measured inputs are within the caps.
3. `YieldBudget` decides when the interpreter awaits a 1 ms timer.
4. Local: `evaluate_local()` → `Outcome`.
5. Activity: `cel_client.evaluate_remote()` sends a `cel.evaluate.v1` request to the evaluator.

**Isolation** (`apps/cel_evaluator`, spec §5.7). Compose only in 2a; the Kubernetes transport is deferred (decision
13).
- The process:
  - its own image, containing only the runtime, protobuf and `engine.cel`;
  - UID 10002, read-only root, `cap_drop: ALL`, `no-new-privileges`;
  - `network_mode: none`; the socket is on a volume shared only with the CEL worker.
- Startup refuses:
  - a non-allow-listed environment;
  - a missing cgroup memory limit, or fewer than one slot;
  - a non-Linux host;
  - a profile mismatch.
- The zygote:
  - is single-threaded asyncio, one request per connection, and warms the runtime before forking;
  - admits N running plus N queued, then answers `busy`;
  - forks per request. The child points its standard streams at `/dev/null`, closes every other descriptor, applies
    the rlimits, re-checks types and order safety, evaluates, and writes one frame.
- How a child ends becomes the outcome:
  - wall-clock kill: `timeout`;
  - SIGXCPU, or SIGKILL at the hard limit: `cpu_limit`;
  - `MemoryError`: `memory_limit`;
  - any other signal: `evaluation_crashed`.
- Frames: malformed, oversized or silent frames close the connection. Profile mismatches fail closed.

## Gates (spec §5.9) and where they run

| Gate | What passes | Tests | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Semantics | 82 pinned cases: the spike's 77, 3 of its 4 probes, 2 map cases, including the 3 checker deviations pinned as PARSE; plus every `fn-1` function | `test_gate_semantics.py`, `test_functions.py` | everywhere |
| 2 Hostile input | 8 compile bombs are compile errors in-process; 7 local-class probes at the caps end in a value or an error; 5 activity-class probes are contained by the child (budget, memory limit, value) and the zygote survives | `test_gate_hostile.py`, `tests/apps/cel_evaluator/test_limits.py` | in-process half everywhere; child half Linux |
| 3 Determinism | 8 processes with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` and shuffled map insertion give one result for 11 expressions (`sortedKeys`, canonical maps, error absorption, `fn-1`) | `test_gate_determinism.py` | everywhere |
| 4 Temporal replay | — | 2a-3 | 2a-3 |
| 5 Classifier | every reject route, the local subset, every activity reason, the typed-path encoding, has-on-typed | `test_classify.py`, `test_validate_cel.py`, `test_typed_paths.py` | everywhere |
| 6 Estimator soundness | Hypothesis: 300 random allow-list expressions (about 30% local, most with 2+ comprehensions), each on a random input within caps and on the densest input within caps: iterations and result size ≤ stored bounds, the budget never fires; model ≤ 8 × JSON + 8; peak RSS growth of the worst local cases at the caps (chains included), each in a fresh process with its peak reset (Linux), ≤ 1.5 × its bound and ≤ 1.5 × 4 MiB; a control allocation must be seen and every probe must grow, so a blind measurement fails. Mutation-checked: dropping the 8× factor or the accumulator charge fails it | `test_gate_estimator.py` | everywhere; RSS authoritative on Linux |
| 7 Local cost | p99/max per local evaluation at caps ≤ 0.2 s (tripwire); a workflow task's CPU between two yields ≤ 1 s for a mixed load and one adversarial load per work charge (Python calls, text, search, regex, equality, conversion), each at its heaviest local form on inputs worst within the caps; results written to `$DEWPOINT_CEL_GATE_RESULTS/cost.json`. Mutation-checked: removing any charge, or the work threshold, fails it | `test_gate_cost.py` | everywhere; the CI artifact per profile. Workflow-task half: 2a-3 |

Evaluator isolation (spec §10): `test_server.py` (framing, `busy`, fail-closed profiles, timeout/crash/CPU
mapping), `test_isolation.py` (import set, environment refusal), `test_config_capacity.py` (N from cgroup files) and
`test_limits.py` (real rlimits, Linux). The CI job `cel-gates` runs all of the above in `python:3.12-slim-bookworm`.

## File structure

```
backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/
  profile.py        (modify) runtime pin, fn/classifier versions, LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE build constant   Task 1
  proto/            vendored cel.expr protos + protoc output + README                              Task 1
  types.py          the closed set of declared CEL types; value conformance                        Task 1
  runtime.py        THE adapter: parse, compile_checked, evaluate (only cel_expr_python importer)  Task 1, 2
  functions.py      fn-1: declarations, metadata, pure implementations                              Task 2
  ast.py            walkers, scoping, select chains, depth, JSON types of checked types             Task 3
  caps.py           runtime caps and the size model                                                  Task 4
  estimate.py       bound estimator = local allow-list (iterations, bytes, work)                     Task 4
  classify.py       reject rules (order) and local/activity classification                           Task 5
  guards.py         presence facts from has() for cel.conditional_ref                               Task 5
  record.py         ExpressionRecord (stored per version) and projections                            Task 8
  bind.py           ScopeView, projection, binding checks, measurement                               Task 10
  route.py          routing and the yield policy                                                     Task 10
  canonical.py      output canonicalization (§5.8)                                                   Task 11
  evaluate.py       outcomes and local evaluation                                                    Task 11
  ipc.py            cel.evaluate.v1 frames, request validation, evaluate_request                     Task 13
backend/src/dewpoint/sdk/manifest.py  (modify) exact output contract: required fields, dump_output, no model serializers  Task 7
backend/src/dewpoint/engine/graph/
  values.py         (modify) item/index roots, CEL_KEYWORDS                                          Task 6
  schemas.py        (modify) declared_optional: where the schema says a field may be absent          Task 8
  validate.py       (modify) item/index scoping, reserved names; CEL values via cel_check           Task 6, 8
  cel_check.py      publish-time CEL: environment, references, guards, result type, record           Task 8
backend/src/dewpoint/apps/
  workflow_ops.py   (modify) store expressions at publish                                             Task 9
  api/routes/workflows.py (modify) validate reports each expression's mode                            Task 9
  cel_client.py     evaluator client + the cel.evaluate activity core                                Task 14
  cel_evaluator/    __init__, config, capacity, child, server, __main__                               Task 14
backend/tests/engine/conftest.py            engine tests need no database                             Task 1
backend/tests/engine/cel/                   unit tests and gates                                      Tasks 1–5, 10–13
backend/tests/engine/graph/test_validate_cel.py                                                       Task 8
backend/tests/apps/cel_evaluator/                                                                     Task 14
deploy/docker/cel-evaluator.Dockerfile, deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml, .github/workflows/ci.yml   Task 15
docs/operations/cel-evaluator.md, README.md                                                           Task 16
(spec revision 5.3 lands with this plan in the docs PR, before Task 1)
```

## Suggested checkpoints (the owner's milestone reviews)

1. **After Task 5:** the typed-path proof, `fn-1`, AST and chains, estimator, classifier and guards. Everything
   publish needs, without the validator yet.
2. **After Task 9:** CEL in the validator, `item`/`index`, and stored records.
3. **After Task 13:** run-time binding, routing, evaluation, the in-process gates and the protocol.
4. **After Task 16:** the evaluator service, deployment, CI and docs. Then the whole-branch review.

## Branching

As in 2a-1:
1. This plan and spec revision 5.3 land in a docs-only PR from `main`, branch `docs/engine-2a2-plan`.
2. After it merges, implementation starts on `feat/engine-2a2` from the updated `main`.

The spec is amended in the docs PR, not at the end of the implementation. Task 16 only adds the operations runbook
and the README note, which describe code that exists by then.

---

### Task 1: Runtime pin, vendored protos, the adapter, and the typed-path proof

The go/no-go result becomes a test suite. Everything later relies on these behaviours, and a runtime upgrade that
changes one fails here first.

**Files:**
- Modify: `backend/pyproject.toml`, `backend/uv.lock` (dependencies, lint and type config, import contracts)
- Modify: `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/profile.py`
- Create: `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/` (`__init__.py`, `README.md`, `checked.proto`, `syntax.proto`,
  `checked_pb2.py`, `checked_pb2.pyi`, `syntax_pb2.py`, `syntax_pb2.pyi`)
- Create: `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/types.py`, `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/runtime.py`
- Create: `backend/tests/engine/conftest.py`, `backend/tests/engine/cel/__init__.py`,
  `backend/tests/engine/cel/test_protos.py`, `test_profile.py`, `test_runtime.py`, `test_typed_paths.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `dewpoint.engine.ENGINE_ABI` (2a-1), `CURRENT_CEL_PROFILE` (2a-1; same value).
- Produces:
  - `types`: `DYN`, `INT`, `STRING`, `BOOL`, `MAP`, `LIST`, `LIST_OF_MAPS`, `LIST_OF_STRINGS`, `LIST_OF_BOOLS`,
    `LIST_OF_LISTS`, `ELEMENT`, `SIGNATURES`, `INT64_MIN`, `INT64_MAX`, `list_of(element) -> str`,
    `conforms(signature, value) -> bool`.
  - `runtime`:
    - `CompileError(problems: list[str])`;
    - `Compiled(checked: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr, program)`;
    - `RawResult(kind: "value" | "error" | "aborted", value, type_name, message)`;
    - `parse(expr) -> syntax_pb2.ParsedExpr`;
    - `compile_checked(expr, declarations: Mapping[str, str]) -> Compiled`;
    - `evaluate(compiled, bindings) -> RawResult`.
  - `profile`: `RUNTIME_DISTRIBUTION`, `RUNTIME_VERSION`, `FUNCTION_LIBRARY`, `CLASSIFIER`, `CURRENT_CEL_PROFILE`,
    `LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE: str | None = None`, `profile_of(runtime_version) -> str`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Lint, type and import rules for the new code**

In `backend/pyproject.toml`, add `extend-exclude` to `[tool.ruff]`:

```toml
[tool.ruff]
line-length = 120
target-version = "py312"
extend-exclude = ["src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/*_pb2.py", "src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/*_pb2.pyi"]
```

After the `[tool.mypy]` table, add:

```toml
[[tool.mypy.overrides]]
# Vendored protoc output: the .pyi stubs next to it are what mypy reads (see engine/cel/proto/README.md).
module = ["dewpoint.engine.cel.proto.*"]
ignore_errors = true

[[tool.mypy.overrides]]
# cel-expr-python ships a .pyi but no py.typed marker; only engine.cel.runtime imports it.
module = ["cel_expr_python", "cel_expr_python.*"]
ignore_missing_imports = true
```

At the end of the import-linter contracts, add:

```toml
[[tool.importlinter.contracts]]
name = "only the CEL runtime adapter imports cel-expr-python"
type = "forbidden"
source_modules = ["dewpoint"]
forbidden_modules = ["cel_expr_python"]
ignore_imports = ["dewpoint.engine.cel.runtime -> cel_expr_python"]
allow_indirect_imports = true

[[tool.importlinter.contracts]]
name = "engine.cel reads no schemas and no graph"
type = "forbidden"
source_modules = ["dewpoint.engine.cel"]
forbidden_modules = ["dewpoint.engine.graph", "dewpoint.engine.registry", "jsonschema", "pydantic"]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Vendor the protos**

The `.proto` files come from the spike branch, which copied them from cel-spec `b0b1083`. The protoc output is
regenerated with grpcio-tools 1.84.0, the version the spike used. The spike's `spikes/cel/.venv` has it installed
locally; `uvx --from grpcio-tools==1.84.0` works too (a download: confirm first). The `.py` output must be
byte-identical to the spike's.

```bash
cd backend && P=src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto && mkdir -p $P && \
for f in checked syntax; do git show spike/cel-evaluation:spikes/cel/protos/cel/expr/$f.proto > $P/$f.proto; done && \
tmp=$(mktemp -d) && mkdir -p "$tmp/cel/expr" && cp $P/*.proto "$tmp/cel/expr/" && \
../spikes/cel/.venv/bin/python -m grpc_tools.protoc -I "$tmp" --python_out="$tmp" --pyi_out="$tmp" \
  cel/expr/syntax.proto cel/expr/checked.proto && \
for f in checked syntax; do git show spike/cel-evaluation:spikes/cel/gen/cel/expr/${f}_pb2.py | cmp - "$tmp/cel/expr/${f}_pb2.py"; done && \
cp "$tmp"/cel/expr/*_pb2.py* $P/ && \
sed -i.bak 's/^from cel\.expr import syntax_pb2/from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import syntax_pb2/' \
  $P/checked_pb2.py $P/checked_pb2.pyi && rm src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/*.bak && \
git hash-object $P/checked.proto $P/syntax.proto && grep -c '^from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import' $P/checked_pb2.py*
```

Expected: `cmp` prints nothing, and the blob hashes are `0105b93adafadef166846f03710bc44cdc7f337f` and
`00635e664cdb8eaa3bacfc4fc169ceb291b3ccc7`. Each `checked_pb2` file has one patched import.

Create `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/__init__.py`:

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Vendored cel-spec protos (`cel.expr`), Apache-2.0, from google/cel-spec commit
b0b10835ca4d31a1a32b86e2e25d66bb9dd8f042. See README.md for provenance and regeneration."""
```

Create `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/README.md`. The `.proto` files keep upstream's two lines of trailing
whitespace; a root `.gitattributes` exempts them from whitespace checks (added at checkpoint 1):

````markdown
# Vendored cel-spec protos

The CEL classifier reads checked ASTs (`google.protobuf.Any` wrapping `cel.expr.CheckedExpr`) with these messages
(spec §5.5). They are vendored, not installed, so the decoded format can only change on purpose.

| File | Source | Git blob |
|---|---|---|
| `checked.proto` | [google/cel-spec](https://github.com/google/cel-spec) `proto/cel/expr/checked.proto` at `b0b10835ca4d31a1a32b86e2e25d66bb9dd8f042` | `0105b93adafadef166846f03710bc44cdc7f337f` |
| `syntax.proto` | same commit, `proto/cel/expr/syntax.proto` | `00635e664cdb8eaa3bacfc4fc169ceb291b3ccc7` |

License: Apache-2.0 (the headers in the `.proto` files; the same license as Dewpoint). `tests/engine/cel/test_protos.py`
checks the blob hashes.
The `.proto` files are never reformatted: `checked.proto` has two lines of upstream trailing whitespace, so
`.gitattributes` exempts these files from whitespace checks rather than changing their hashes.

`*_pb2.py` and `*_pb2.pyi` are protoc output from grpcio-tools 1.84.0 (protobuf gencode 7.35.1). The only edit is the
import of `syntax_pb2` in `checked_pb2.py` and `checked_pb2.pyi`, from `cel.expr` to this package. To regenerate, from
`backend/`:

```bash
tmp=$(mktemp -d) && mkdir -p "$tmp/cel/expr" && cp src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/*.proto "$tmp/cel/expr/" && \
uvx --from grpcio-tools==1.84.0 python -m grpc_tools.protoc -I "$tmp" --python_out="$tmp" --pyi_out="$tmp" \
  cel/expr/syntax.proto cel/expr/checked.proto && \
cp "$tmp"/cel/expr/*_pb2.py* src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/ && \
sed -i.bak 's/^from cel\.expr import syntax_pb2/from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import syntax_pb2/' \
  src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/checked_pb2.py src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/checked_pb2.pyi && \
rm src/dewpoint/engine/cel/proto/*.bak
```

A new runtime pin, a new protobuf major version or new protos is a new CEL profile (spec §5.1): run the §5.9 gates.
````

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""The vendored cel-spec protos are the pinned ones (spec §5.5): git blob hashes of the upstream files at
google/cel-spec commit b0b10835ca4d31a1a32b86e2e25d66bb9dd8f042."""

import hashlib
from pathlib import Path

import dewpoint.engine.cel.proto as proto_pkg
from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import checked_pb2, syntax_pb2

PINNED = {
    "checked.proto": "0105b93adafadef166846f03710bc44cdc7f337f",
    "syntax.proto": "00635e664cdb8eaa3bacfc4fc169ceb291b3ccc7",
}


def _git_blob(data: bytes) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha1(b"blob %d\0" % len(data) + data, usedforsecurity=False).hexdigest()


def test_vendored_proto_sources_match_the_pinned_commit() -> None:
    here = Path(proto_pkg.__file__).parent
    assert {name: _git_blob((here / name).read_bytes()) for name in PINNED} == PINNED


def test_generated_modules_describe_the_same_files() -> None:
    assert checked_pb2.DESCRIPTOR.name == "cel/expr/checked.proto"
    assert syntax_pb2.DESCRIPTOR.name == "cel/expr/syntax.proto"
    assert checked_pb2.CheckedExpr.DESCRIPTOR.full_name == "cel.expr.CheckedExpr"
```

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import importlib.metadata

from dewpoint.engine import ENGINE_ABI
from dewpoint.engine.cel import profile


def test_the_profile_names_the_installed_runtime() -> None:
    assert importlib.metadata.version(profile.RUNTIME_DISTRIBUTION) == profile.RUNTIME_VERSION
    assert profile.CURRENT_CEL_PROFILE == profile.profile_of(profile.RUNTIME_VERSION) == "cel-cpp-0.1.3/fn-1/cls-1"


def test_local_evaluation_is_a_build_constant_tied_to_the_engine_abi() -> None:
    """Tripwire (spec §5.9 rollout): LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE is part of engine_abi. Changing it without bumping ENGINE_ABI
    would let an open run replay on a build that routes its CEL differently. Update both, then this pair."""
    assert (ENGINE_ABI, profile.LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE) == (1, None)
```

`backend/tests/engine/cel/test_runtime.py`:

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import pytest

from dewpoint.engine.cel import runtime
from dewpoint.engine.cel import types as T


def test_compile_gives_a_checked_ast_and_evaluates() -> None:
    compiled = runtime.compile_checked("x + 1", {"x": T.INT})
    assert compiled.checked.expr.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "call_expr"
    assert runtime.evaluate(compiled, {"x": 2}) == runtime.RawResult("value", 3, "INT")


def test_compile_errors_carry_every_checker_message() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(runtime.CompileError) as e:
        runtime.compile_checked("x + 'a' + y", {"x": T.INT})
    assert e.value.problems[0].startswith("1:3: found no matching overload")
    assert any("undeclared reference to 'y'" in p for p in e.value.problems)


def test_parse_only_keeps_has_as_a_test_only_select() -> None:
    parsed = runtime.parse("has(a.b)")
    assert parsed.expr.select_expr.test_only


def test_error_values_and_aborts_are_distinguished_from_strings() -> None:
    div = runtime.compile_checked("1 / x", {"x": T.INT})
    assert runtime.evaluate(div, {"x": 0}) == runtime.RawResult("error", message="INVALID_ARGUMENT: divide by zero")
    text = runtime.compile_checked("'INVALID_ARGUMENT: x'", {})
    assert runtime.evaluate(text, {}).kind == "value"
    budget = runtime.compile_checked("l.all(a, l.all(b, true))", {"l": T.LIST})
    result = runtime.evaluate(budget, {"l": list(range(200))})
    assert result.kind == "aborted" and "Iteration budget exceeded" in result.message


def test_only_supported_signatures_are_declared() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unsupported CEL type"):
        runtime.compile_checked("x", {"x": "list(dyn)"})
```

`backend/tests/engine/cel/test_typed_paths.py`: the go/no-go, codified.

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Go/no-go proof of the typed-path encoding (spec §5.3, decision 7) against the pinned runtime.

Roots are `map<string, dyn>`; an always-available array path is also declared as a qualified identifier. These tests
pin every behaviour the classifier and binder rely on. If one fails after a runtime upgrade, the profile is not safe
to ship until the encoding is re-proved."""

from typing import Any

import pytest

from dewpoint.engine.cel import runtime
from dewpoint.engine.cel import types as T
from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import checked_pb2

DECLS = {
    "steps": T.MAP,
    "trigger": T.MAP,
    "loops": T.MAP,
    "steps.get_devices.output.devices": T.LIST_OF_MAPS,
    "steps.get_site.output.tags": T.LIST_OF_STRINGS,
    "steps.if.output.items": T.LIST_OF_MAPS,
    "trigger.sites": T.LIST_OF_MAPS,
    "loops.outer.item.ports": T.LIST_OF_MAPS,
}
DEVICES = [{"name": "ap1"}, {"name": "ap2"}]
DATA: dict[str, Any] = {
    "steps": {"get_devices": {"output": {"devices": DEVICES}}, "get_site": {"output": {"tags": ["a", "b"]}}},
    "trigger": {"sites": [{"name": "paris"}]},
    "loops": {"outer": {"item": {"ports": [{"enabled": True}]}}},
    "steps.get_devices.output.devices": DEVICES,
    "steps.get_site.output.tags": ["a", "b"],
    "steps.if.output.items": [],
    "trigger.sites": [{"name": "paris"}],
    "loops.outer.item.ports": [{"enabled": True}],
}


def _checked(expr: str) -> checked_pb2.CheckedExpr:
    return runtime.compile_checked(expr, DECLS).checked


def _ranges(c: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr) -> list[str]:
    out: list[str] = []
    stack = [c.expr]
    while stack:
        e = stack.pop()
        kind = e.WhichOneof("expr_kind")
        if kind == "comprehension_expr":
            ce = e.comprehension_expr
            out.append(c.type_map[ce.iter_range.id].WhichOneof("type_kind"))
            stack += [ce.iter_range, ce.accu_init, ce.loop_condition, ce.loop_step, ce.result]
        elif kind == "call_expr":
            stack += list(e.call_expr.args) + ([e.call_expr.target] if e.call_expr.HasField("target") else [])
        elif kind == "select_expr":
            stack.append(e.select_expr.operand)
        elif kind == "list_expr":
            stack += list(e.list_expr.elements)
    return sorted(out)


def _idents(c: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr) -> set[str]:
    return {r.name for r in c.reference_map.values() if r.name}


def _eval(expr: str, data: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> runtime.RawResult:
    return runtime.evaluate(runtime.compile_checked(expr, DECLS), data or DATA)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "expr",
    [
        "steps.get_devices.output.devices.map(d, d.name)",
        "steps.get_site.output.tags.filter(t, t.startsWith('a'))",
        "trigger.sites.exists(s, s.name == 'paris')",
        "loops.outer.item.ports.all(p, p.enabled)",
        "steps.if.output.items.map(x, x)",  # a reserved word (not in/true/false/null) is a legal segment
        "(steps.get_devices).output.devices.map(d, d)",  # parentheses don't change the resolution
    ],
)
def test_the_longest_declared_qualified_name_types_the_range_as_a_list(expr: str) -> None:
    assert _ranges(_checked(expr)) == ["list_type"]


def test_undeclared_paths_and_index_syntax_stay_dyn() -> None:
    assert _ranges(_checked("steps.other.output.items.map(i, i)")) == ["dyn"]
    assert _ranges(_checked("steps['get_devices'].output.devices.map(d, d)")) == ["dyn"]


def test_a_nested_list_under_an_element_stays_dyn() -> None:
    assert _ranges(_checked("steps.get_devices.output.devices.map(d, d.ports.map(p, p))")) == ["dyn", "list_type"]


def test_the_checker_resolves_qualified_names_to_the_declared_identifier() -> None:
    assert "steps.get_devices.output.devices" in _idents(_checked("steps.get_devices.output.devices.size()"))
    assert _idents(_checked("steps.get_devices.output")) >= {"steps"}  # a prefix goes through the root


def test_typed_paths_catch_type_errors_at_check_time() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(runtime.CompileError, match="no matching overload"):
        _checked("steps.get_devices.output.devices + 1")


def test_has_on_the_root_and_on_prefixes_works() -> None:
    assert _eval("has(steps.get_devices)").value is True
    assert _eval("has(steps.missing)").value is False
    assert _eval("has(steps.get_devices.output)").value is True


def test_has_on_a_declared_typed_path_collapses_into_the_identifier() -> None:
    """The checker rewrites the test-only select into the identifier: the result is the list, not a bool. The
    classifier rejects this form (cel.has_on_typed_path); these assertions pin why."""
    c = _checked("has(steps.get_devices.output.devices)")
    assert c.expr.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "ident_expr"
    assert c.type_map[c.expr.id].WhichOneof("type_kind") == "list_type"
    assert _eval("has(steps.get_devices.output.devices)").value == DEVICES
    with pytest.raises(runtime.CompileError):
        _checked("has(steps.get_devices.output.devices) && true")
    parsed = runtime.parse("has(steps.get_devices.output.devices)")
    assert parsed.expr.id == c.expr.id and parsed.expr.select_expr.test_only  # ids match: detectable


def test_binding_both_the_root_and_the_qualified_identifier() -> None:
    assert _eval("steps.get_devices.output.devices.map(d, d.name)").value == ["ap1", "ap2"]
    conflict = {**DATA, "steps.get_devices.output.devices": [{"name": "qualified"}]}
    assert _eval("steps.get_devices.output.devices.map(d, d.name)", conflict).value == ["qualified"]
    missing = {k: v for k, v in DATA.items() if not k.startswith("steps.get_devices")}
    missing["steps"] = {}
    assert _eval("has(steps.get_devices) ? steps.get_devices.output.devices.size() : 0", missing).value == 0


def test_an_iteration_variable_shadows_a_root_in_checker_and_runtime_alike() -> None:
    expr = "[{'get_devices': {'output': {'devices': {'b': 1, 'a': 2}}}}].map(steps, steps.get_devices.output.devices)"
    c = _checked(expr)
    assert "steps.get_devices.output.devices" not in _idents(c)
    assert _eval(expr).value == [{"b": 1, "a": 2}]


def test_the_runtime_checks_declared_types_deeply_at_binding() -> None:
    bad = {**DATA, "steps.get_devices.output.devices": {"b": {"name": 1}}}
    result = _eval("steps.get_devices.output.devices.map(d, d)", bad)
    assert result.kind == "error" and "Unexpected value type" in result.message


def test_loop_is_a_reserved_identifier() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(runtime.CompileError, match="reserved identifier: loop"):
        runtime.compile_checked("loop.item", {"loop": T.MAP})


@pytest.mark.parametrize("word", ["in", "true", "false", "null"])
def test_four_keywords_cannot_be_selected_as_fields(word: str) -> None:
    with pytest.raises(runtime.CompileError):
        runtime.compile_checked(f"steps.{word}.output", {"steps": T.MAP})


def test_serialization_is_not_deterministic_but_the_decoded_ast_is() -> None:
    expr = "steps.get_devices.output.devices.map(d, d.name)"
    a, b = _checked(expr), _checked(expr)
    assert a == b
    assert a.SerializeToString(deterministic=True) == b.SerializeToString(deterministic=True)


def test_many_declarations_compile_quickly() -> None:
    import time

    decls = {"steps": T.MAP, **{f"steps.s{i}.output.items": T.LIST_OF_MAPS for i in range(2000)}}
    started = time.perf_counter()
    for i in range(50):
        runtime.compile_checked(f"steps.s{i}.output.items.map(x, x.v)", decls)
    assert time.perf_counter() - started < 5.0
```

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""The CEL profile (spec §5.1): runtime pin / function library / classifier rules. Changing any part is a new
profile, shipped only after the §5.9 gates pass for it."""

RUNTIME_DISTRIBUTION = "cel-expr-python"
RUNTIME_VERSION = "0.1.3"
FUNCTION_LIBRARY = "fn-1"
# cls-1 is the classifier as plan 2a-2 first ships it. Its rules changed while 2a-2 was built (caps, retained
# accumulators, work charges, regex eligibility) without a new version: no published version had classified an
# expression under it, because 2a-1 refused CEL at publish (cel.unavailable) and nothing had been released. Once 2a-2
# is merged, any change to the caps, the estimator or the classifier's limits is cls-2.
CLASSIFIER = "cls-1"
CURRENT_CEL_PROFILE = f"cel-cpp-{RUNTIME_VERSION}/{FUNCTION_LIBRARY}/{CLASSIFIER}"

# The one profile this worker build evaluates in-process, or None (spec §5.9 rollout). A build constant, never a
# setting: it is part of engine_abi, so changing it requires bumping ENGINE_ABI (tests/engine/cel/test_profile.py).
# Stays None until gates 1-7 pass, including the workflow-task gates that 2a-3 adds.
LOCAL_CEL_PROFILE: str | None = None


def profile_of(runtime_version: str) -> str:
    return f"cel-cpp-{runtime_version}/{FUNCTION_LIBRARY}/{CLASSIFIER}"
```

Create `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/types.py`:

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""The CEL types Dewpoint declares (spec §5.3), as runtime signatures, and the value checks behind them.

The set is closed: an evaluator request can only name these types, and binding checks every value against its
declared type before any CEL runs."""

from typing import Any

DYN = "dyn"
INT = "int"
STRING = "string"
BOOL = "bool"
MAP = "map<string, dyn>"
LIST = "list<dyn>"
LIST_OF_MAPS = "list<map<string, dyn>>"
LIST_OF_STRINGS = "list<string>"
LIST_OF_BOOLS = "list<bool>"
LIST_OF_LISTS = "list<list<dyn>>"
ELEMENT: dict[str, str] = {
    LIST: DYN,
    LIST_OF_MAPS: MAP,
    LIST_OF_STRINGS: STRING,
    LIST_OF_BOOLS: BOOL,
    LIST_OF_LISTS: LIST,
}
SIGNATURES = frozenset({DYN, INT, STRING, BOOL, MAP, *ELEMENT})
INT64_MIN, INT64_MAX = -(2**63), 2**63 - 1


def list_of(element: str) -> str:
    """The list signature for an element signature; unsupported elements become `list<dyn>`."""
    for sig, elem in ELEMENT.items():
        if elem == element:
            return sig
    return LIST


def conforms(signature: str, value: Any) -> bool:
    """True when `value` (plain JSON) has the declared type. Unknown signatures never conform."""
    if signature == DYN:
        return True
    if signature == INT:
        return isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool)
    if signature == STRING:
        return isinstance(value, str)
    if signature == BOOL:
        return isinstance(value, bool)
    if signature == MAP:
        return isinstance(value, dict) and all(isinstance(k, str) for k in value)
    element = ELEMENT.get(signature)
    if element is None or not isinstance(value, list):
        return False
    return element == DYN or all(conforms(element, v) for v in value)
```

Create `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/runtime.py`. Task 2 registers `fn-1`; this version declares no functions:

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""The CEL runtime adapter (spec §5.1). This is the only module that imports cel-expr-python.

Four operations: parse, compile with a typed environment (giving the checked AST), evaluate, and report errors.
Checked ASTs are decoded with the vendored cel-spec protos. The runtime's own serialization is not deterministic
(protobuf map order), so nothing hashes or stores it: callers keep the decoded message or facts derived from it."""

import re
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

from cel_expr_python import cel as _cel
from google.protobuf import any_pb2

from dewpoint.engine.cel import types
from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import checked_pb2, syntax_pb2

_PROBLEM = re.compile(r"ERROR: <input>:(\d+):(\d+): ([^\n]*)")


class CompileError(ValueError):
    """Parse or type-check failure. `problems` holds the checker's messages, first one first."""

    def __init__(self, problems: list[str]) -> None:
        super().__init__(problems[0] if problems else "invalid expression")
        self.problems = problems


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Compiled:
    checked: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr
    program: Any  # the runtime's Expression; evaluate() is its only use


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RawResult:
    """What the runtime returned. kind: `value` (plain Python value, runtime type name), `error` (a CEL error value)
    or `aborted` (the runtime raised, e.g. the iteration budget)."""

    kind: str
    value: Any = None
    type_name: str = ""
    message: str = ""


def _problems(error: Exception) -> list[str]:
    text = str(error)
    found = [f"{line}:{col}: {msg}" for line, col, msg in _PROBLEM.findall(text)]
    return found or [text.splitlines()[0] if text else type(error).__name__]


def _type(signature: str) -> Any:
    if signature not in types.SIGNATURES:
        raise ValueError(f"unsupported CEL type {signature!r}")
    return _cel.Type(signature)


def _env(declarations: Mapping[str, str]) -> Any:
    return _cel.NewEnv(variables={name: _type(sig) for name, sig in declarations.items()})


def _unpack(serialized: bytes, message: Any) -> Any:
    wrapper = any_pb2.Any()
    wrapper.ParseFromString(serialized)
    if not wrapper.Unpack(message):
        raise CompileError([f"unexpected AST payload {wrapper.type_url}"])
    return message


def parse(expr: str) -> syntax_pb2.ParsedExpr:
    """Syntax only: no declarations, no type check. Node ids match the checked AST of the same text."""
    try:
        compiled = _env({}).compile(expr, disable_check=True)
    except RuntimeError as e:
        raise CompileError(_problems(e)) from None
    parsed: syntax_pb2.ParsedExpr = _unpack(compiled.serialize(), syntax_pb2.ParsedExpr())
    return parsed


def compile_checked(expr: str, declarations: Mapping[str, str]) -> Compiled:
    try:
        program = _env(declarations).compile(expr)
    except RuntimeError as e:
        raise CompileError(_problems(e)) from None
    checked: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr = _unpack(program.serialize(), checked_pb2.CheckedExpr())
    return Compiled(checked, program)


def evaluate(compiled: Compiled, bindings: Mapping[str, Any]) -> RawResult:
    try:
        value = compiled.program.eval(data=dict(bindings))
    except RuntimeError as e:  # the runtime aborts (e.g. "Iteration budget exceeded") instead of returning an error
        return RawResult("aborted", message=str(e))
    type_name = value.type().name()
    if type_name == "ERROR":
        return RawResult("error", message=str(value.plain_value()))
    return RawResult("value", value.plain_value(), type_name)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/functions.py`:

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Function library `fn-1` (spec §5.4): declarations, metadata and pure Python implementations.

Implementations never read time, randomness, I/O or locale. A failure raises ValueError, which the runtime turns
into a CEL error value (so `||` and `&&` can absorb it like any other error)."""

import ipaddress
import re
from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Literal

from dewpoint.engine.cel import types as T

LIBRARY = "fn-1"
MAX_ARGUMENT = 64  # code points: IP, CIDR and MAC arguments are short; longer input is an error, not work
_MAC_LAYOUTS = (
    re.compile(r"[0-9a-f]{12}"),
    re.compile(r"(?:[0-9a-f]{2}[:-]){5}[0-9a-f]{2}"),
    re.compile(r"(?:[0-9a-f]{4}\.){2}[0-9a-f]{4}"),
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Overload:
    id: str
    result: str
    params: tuple[str, ...]
    impl: Callable[..., Any]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Function:
    name: str
    overloads: tuple[Overload, ...]
    cost: Literal["constant", "n log n"]
    output: str  # the output bound, as documented
    takes_map: bool
    deterministic: bool = True


def _fail(function: str, detail: str) -> ValueError:
    return ValueError(f"invalid_argument: {function}: {detail}")


def _short(function: str, text: str) -> str:
    if len(text) > MAX_ARGUMENT:
        raise _fail(function, f"argument longer than {MAX_ARGUMENT} characters")
    return text


def sorted_keys(m: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
    return sorted(m)  # Python orders str by code point, as the spec requires


def as_list(value: list[Any]) -> list[Any]:
    return value  # the runtime dispatches only list arguments here; anything else has no matching overload


def ip_in_cidr(ip: str, cidr: str) -> bool:
    try:
        address = ipaddress.ip_address(_short("ipInCidr", ip))
        network = ipaddress.ip_network(_short("ipInCidr", cidr), strict=False)
    except ValueError as e:
        raise _fail("ipInCidr", str(e)) from None
    return address.version == network.version and address in network


def cidr_contains(outer: str, inner: str) -> bool:
    try:
        a = ipaddress.ip_network(_short("cidrContains", outer), strict=False)
        b = ipaddress.ip_network(_short("cidrContains", inner), strict=False)
    except ValueError as e:
        raise _fail("cidrContains", str(e)) from None
    if a.version != b.version:
        return False
    return b.subnet_of(a)  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # same version, checked above


def mac_normalize(text: str) -> str:
    lowered = _short("macNormalize", text).lower()
    if not any(layout.fullmatch(lowered) for layout in _MAC_LAYOUTS):
        raise _fail("macNormalize", "not a MAC address")
    return re.sub(r"[:.-]", "", lowered)


def mac_oui(text: str) -> str:
    return mac_normalize(text)[:6]


FUNCTIONS: tuple[Function, ...] = (
    Function(
        "sortedKeys",
        (Overload("sortedKeys_map", T.LIST_OF_STRINGS, (T.MAP,), sorted_keys),),
        "n log n",
        "≤ input",
        True,
    ),
    Function("asList", (Overload("asList_list", T.LIST, (T.LIST,), as_list),), "constant", "= input", False),
    Function(
        "ipInCidr",
        (Overload("ipInCidr_string_string", T.BOOL, (T.STRING, T.STRING), ip_in_cidr),),
        "constant",
        "bool",
        False,
    ),
    Function(
        "cidrContains",
        (Overload("cidrContains_string_string", T.BOOL, (T.STRING, T.STRING), cidr_contains),),
        "constant",
        "bool",
        False,
    ),
    Function(
        "macNormalize",
        (Overload("macNormalize_string", T.STRING, (T.STRING,), mac_normalize),),
        "constant",
        "12 code points",
        False,
    ),
    Function(
        "macOui", (Overload("macOui_string", T.STRING, (T.STRING,), mac_oui),), "constant", "6 code points", False
    ),
)
NAMES = frozenset(f.name for f in FUNCTIONS)
OVERLOAD_IDS = frozenset(o.id for f in FUNCTIONS for o in f.overloads)
```

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**

- Produces:

  - `ast.Chain(path, presence, expr_id, ident)` with a `.root` property;
  - `children(e)`, `walk(e)`, `operands(e)`, `scoped(e, scope)`;
  - `chain_path(e, scope) -> Path | None`, `chains(root) -> list[Chain]`, `global_idents(root) -> list[str]`,
    `called_functions(root) -> set[str]`;
  - `comprehension_depth(root) -> int`, `type_kind(checked, id) -> str`, `overloads(checked, id) -> tuple[str, ...]`;
  - `json_types(t) -> frozenset[str] | None` (`NON_JSON` for bytes and type values).

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
from dewpoint.engine.cel import ast, runtime
from dewpoint.engine.cel import types as T

ROOTS = {"steps": T.MAP, "trigger": T.MAP, "vars": T.MAP}


def _checked(expr: str, extra: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> runtime.Compiled:
    return runtime.compile_checked(expr, {**ROOTS, **(extra or {})})


def _paths(expr: str, extra: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> list[tuple[tuple[str, ...], bool]]:
    return [(c.path, c.presence) for c in ast.chains(_checked(expr, extra).checked.expr)]


def test_chains_are_maximal_and_in_evaluation_order() -> None:
    assert _paths("steps.a.output.x + trigger.b") == [(("steps", "a", "output", "x"), False), (("trigger", "b"), False)]


def test_presence_tests_report_the_tested_field() -> None:
    assert _paths("has(steps.a.output) && steps.a.output.x > 1") == [
        (("steps", "a", "output"), True),
        (("steps", "a", "output", "x"), False),
    ]


def test_typed_identifiers_split_back_into_chains() -> None:
    extra = {"steps.a.output.devices": T.LIST_OF_MAPS}
    assert _paths("steps.a.output.devices.map(d, d.name)", extra) == [(("steps", "a", "output", "devices"), False)]


def test_index_ends_a_chain_and_call_results_are_not_chains() -> None:
    assert _paths("steps.a.output.l[0].name") == [(("steps", "a", "output", "l"), False)]
    assert _paths("sortedKeys(vars.m).size()") == [(("vars", "m"), False)]


def test_comprehension_variables_shadow_globals() -> None:
    expr = "trigger.items.map(steps, steps.x) + [vars.y]"
    assert _paths(expr) == [(("trigger", "items"), False), (("vars", "y"), False)]
    assert ast.global_idents(_checked(expr).checked.expr) == ["trigger", "vars"]


def test_comprehension_depth_counts_only_nesting_that_multiplies() -> None:
    def depth(expr: str) -> int:
        return ast.comprehension_depth(_checked(expr).checked.expr)

    assert depth("1 + 2") == 0
    assert depth("[1, 2].map(x, x)") == 1
    assert depth("[1, 2].map(x, x).filter(y, y > 1)") == 1  # the inner one is the range: it runs once
    assert depth("[[1]].map(x, x.map(y, y))") == 2


def test_result_json_types() -> None:
    def types(expr: str) -> frozenset[str] | None:
        c = _checked(expr).checked
        return ast.json_types(c.type_map[c.expr.id])

    assert types("1") == {"integer"} and types("1u") == {"integer"} and types("1.5") == {"number"}
    assert types("'a'") == {"string"} and types("true") == {"boolean"} and types("null") == {"null"}
    assert types("[1]") == {"array"} and types("{'a': 1}") == {"object"}
    assert types("timestamp('2024-01-01T00:00:00Z')") == {"string"}
    assert types("trigger.x") is None
    assert types("b'a'") == ast.NON_JSON and types("type(1)") == ast.NON_JSON
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `backend/src/dewpoint/engine/cel/ast.py`:

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Walks over CEL ASTs (parsed or checked, same node ids): children, scoping, select chains and types.

A *chain* is a run of field selections starting at a global identifier: `steps.a.output.name`. On a checked AST a
typed path arrives as one dotted identifier (`steps.a.output.devices`), which splits back into the same chain.
Comprehension variables shadow globals, as they do in the checker and the runtime."""

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass

from dewpoint.engine.cel.proto import checked_pb2, syntax_pb2

Expr = syntax_pb2.Expr  # the parsed and checked ASTs share the message type
Path = tuple[str, ...]  # (root, field, field, ...)
NON_JSON: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"non-json"})  # a result type with no JSON form (bytes, type values)
_PRIMITIVE_JSON = {1: "boolean", 2: "integer", 3: "integer", 4: "number", 5: "string"}  # BYTES (6) has none


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Chain:
    path: Path  # root first; for a presence test, the tested field is last
    presence: bool  # `has(...)`: only whether the last field exists is observed
    expr_id: int
    ident: str  # the global identifier the chain starts from: a root, or a dotted typed path

    @property
    def root(self) -> str:
        return self.path[0]


def children(e: Expr) -> list[Expr]:
    kind = e.WhichOneof("expr_kind")
    if kind == "select_expr":
        return [e.select_expr.operand]
    if kind == "call_expr":
        target = [e.call_expr.target] if e.call_expr.HasField("target") else []
        return [*target, *e.call_expr.args]
    if kind == "list_expr":
        return list(e.list_expr.elements)
    if kind == "struct_expr":
        out: list[Expr] = []
        for entry in e.struct_expr.entries:
            if entry.HasField("map_key"):
                out.append(entry.map_key)
            out.append(entry.value)
        return out
    if kind == "comprehension_expr":
        ce = e.comprehension_expr
        return [ce.iter_range, ce.accu_init, ce.loop_condition, ce.loop_step, ce.result]
    return []


def walk(e: Expr) -> Iterator[Expr]:
    stack = [e]
    while stack:
        node = stack.pop()
        yield node
        stack.extend(reversed(children(node)))


def operands(e: Expr) -> list[Expr]:
    """A call's operands: the receiver of a member call first, then the arguments."""
    return children(e) if e.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "call_expr" else []


def scoped(e: Expr, scope: frozenset[str]) -> Iterator[tuple[Expr, frozenset[str]]]:
    """Every node with the local names in scope where it is evaluated."""
    stack = [(e, scope)]
    while stack:
        node, names = stack.pop()
        yield node, names
        if node.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "comprehension_expr":
            ce = node.comprehension_expr
            body = names | {ce.iter_var, ce.accu_var}
            parts = [(ce.iter_range, names), (ce.accu_init, names), (ce.loop_condition, body), (ce.loop_step, body)]
            parts.append((ce.result, names | {ce.accu_var}))
            stack.extend(reversed(parts))
        else:
            stack.extend((child, names) for child in reversed(children(node)))


def _chain(e: Expr, scope: frozenset[str]) -> tuple[str, Path] | None:
    fields: list[str] = []
    node = e
    while node.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "select_expr" and not node.select_expr.test_only:
        fields.append(node.select_expr.field)
        node = node.select_expr.operand
    if node.WhichOneof("expr_kind") != "ident_expr":
        return None
    name = node.ident_expr.name
    if name in scope:
        return None
    return name, (*name.split("."), *reversed(fields))


def chain_path(e: Expr, scope: frozenset[str]) -> Path | None:
    """The chain an ident or (non-test) select denotes, or None if it isn't a chain from a global."""
    found = _chain(e, scope)
    return found[1] if found is not None else None


def chains(root: Expr) -> list[Chain]:
    """Maximal chains in evaluation order. A chain's own operands are not reported separately."""
    out: list[Chain] = []
    stack: list[tuple[Expr, frozenset[str]]] = [(root, frozenset())]
    while stack:
        e, scope = stack.pop()
        kind = e.WhichOneof("expr_kind")
        if kind == "select_expr" and e.select_expr.test_only:
            base = _chain(e.select_expr.operand, scope)
            if base is not None:
                out.append(Chain((*base[1], e.select_expr.field), True, e.id, base[0]))
                continue
        elif kind in ("select_expr", "ident_expr"):
            found = _chain(e, scope)
            if found is not None:
                out.append(Chain(found[1], False, e.id, found[0]))
                continue
        if kind == "comprehension_expr":
            ce = e.comprehension_expr
            body = scope | {ce.iter_var, ce.accu_var}
            parts = [(ce.iter_range, scope), (ce.accu_init, scope), (ce.loop_condition, body), (ce.loop_step, body)]
            parts.append((ce.result, scope | {ce.accu_var}))
            stack.extend(reversed(parts))
        else:
            stack.extend((child, scope) for child in reversed(children(e)))
    return out


def global_idents(root: Expr) -> list[str]:
    """Global identifiers the expression reads (dotted for typed paths), sorted, each once."""
    names = {
        e.ident_expr.name
        for e, scope in scoped(root, frozenset())
        if e.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "ident_expr" and e.ident_expr.name not in scope
    }
    return sorted(names)


def called_functions(root: Expr) -> set[str]:
    return {e.call_expr.function for e in walk(root) if e.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "call_expr"}


def comprehension_depth(root: Expr) -> int:
    """Nesting that multiplies work: a comprehension inside another one's condition or step. A comprehension in a
    range, an initial value or a result runs once and doesn't nest."""
    best = 0
    stack = [(root, 0)]
    while stack:
        e, depth = stack.pop()
        if e.WhichOneof("expr_kind") == "comprehension_expr":
            ce = e.comprehension_expr
            depth += 1
            best = max(best, depth)
            stack += [(ce.loop_condition, depth), (ce.loop_step, depth)]
            stack += [(ce.iter_range, depth - 1), (ce.accu_init, depth - 1), (ce.result, depth - 1)]
        else:
            stack += [(child, depth) for child in children(e)]
    return best


def type_kind(checked: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr, expr_id: int) -> str:
    t = checked.type_map.get(expr_id)
    return (t.WhichOneof("type_kind") or "dyn") if t is not None else "dyn"


def overloads(checked: checked_pb2.CheckedExpr, expr_id: int) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    ref = checked.reference_map.get(expr_id)
    return tuple(ref.overload_id) if ref is not None else ()


def json_types(t: checked_pb2.Type | None) -> frozenset[str] | None:
    """The JSON types a checked CEL type can produce after canonicalization (spec §5.8). None: unknown (dyn).
    NON_JSON: a type canonicalization always rejects."""
    if t is None:
        return None
    kind = t.WhichOneof("type_kind")
    if kind in (None, "dyn", "type_param"):
        return None
    if kind == "null":
        return frozenset({"null"})
    if kind == "primitive":
        name = _PRIMITIVE_JSON.get(t.primitive)
        return frozenset({name}) if name else NON_JSON
    if kind == "wrapper":
        name = _PRIMITIVE_JSON.get(t.wrapper)
        return frozenset({name, "null"}) if name else NON_JSON
    if kind == "well_known":
        return frozenset({"string"}) if t.well_known in (2, 3) else None  # TIMESTAMP, DURATION; ANY is unknown
    if kind == "list_type":
        return frozenset({"array"})
    if kind == "map_type":
        return frozenset({"object"})
    return NON_JSON  # type values, errors, functions, messages, abstract types
```
